    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

Consistent temporal precedence (cross-correlation lag=+1, r=0.80–0.94) is observed across all contamination levels, though formal Granger tests at α≤0.50 do not reach FDR-corrected significance at T=11 (power analysis: §X).\\\\\\\"*升级上限：Granger ≠ true causality（Granger 1969），Discussion 一句话声明；双变量模型无法排除未观测混淆因子（详见 Reviewer Concerns 表）。ECE 前向 consensus 与 token_entropy 等效但存在 bidirectional coupling（详见约束 #9）。注意：TY Granger 在 N=11 下不可用于区分真/伪关联（见 Claim 3），核心证据链基于多方法一致性而非单一 TY 背书。**D063 泛化证据**：Table 7 跨 7 conditions（3 architectures × 2 scales × 2 domains）K≥4=5/8-6/8。**Diversity-only subset（排除→ppl pairs）consensus 41/42 = 97.6%**，仅 1.4B s42 的 1 个 diversity pair 未达 K≥4。→ppl pairs 0/14 达 K≥4（结构性失败，非方法缺陷），canary→perplexity 的系统性弱信号作为 explicit finding 报告——diversity collapse 是可预测的下游维度，perplexity 不是。\\\\n2. **Regime-Dependent Predictability**: 可预测性取决于 collapse 维度和 contamination regime——canary→diversity 在差分后仍 robust（6/6 pair × 4/4 seed），但 canary→perplexity **经差分检验确认为伪回归**（0/4 seed, F≈0, p=0.99），未差分时的\\\\\\\"信号\\\\\\\"（F=16-36）完全由共享非平稳趋势驱动（D017）。ECE→perplexity 的反向因果同理存疑。**Claim 2 reframing (D022)**: 从\\\\\\\"不同 α 下 lead time 不同\\\\\\\"升级为\\\\\\\"不同 α 下 **detectability** 不同\\\\\\\"——α=0.25 差分 Granger 0/8 FDR sig vs α=1.0 的 24/24，同一 pair 的统计可检测性随 contamination level 剧变。**D037 更新**：5-point regime map 完整，detectability 在 α=0.50→0.75 间出现阶跃（0/8→3/8 FDR-sig），α=0.75 consensus 11/16 robust。**Evidence strategy (Reviewer 共识)**：Primary = raw drift magnitude dose-response（distinct_1: -14%@α=0 → -40%@α=0.25 → ~-93%@α=1.0），方法无关、不可争议；Secondary = 差分 Granger FDR sig rate vs α，展示 detectability boundary at α~0.75。**不用 naive_xcorr 做 primary**——与 Claim 3 自相矛盾。Predictability regime map 划定可预测/不可预测边界（\\\\\\\"phase diagram\\\\\\\" 需 ≥6 个 α 值 + uncertainty interval 才成立，当前 5 个 α 值下用 \\\\\\\"regime map\\\\\\\"）。\\\\n3. **Method Selection Sensitivity and Regime-Dependent Validity**（升级自 Methodological Rigor；D022 升级候选：method × regime interaction）: 在典型 generation 数（N≈10）下，lead-lag 结论对分析方法和超参数高度敏感，且**同一方法在不同 α regime 下结论翻转**（diff Granger: α=1.0 24/24 FDR sig → α=0.25 0/8）。升级措辞候选：\\\\\\\"Method sensitivity is compounded by regime dependence: the same statistical test applied to the same metric pair can yield significant or null results depending on contamination level\\\\\\\"。**三层证据结构**：**(Layer 1)** naive Pearson 找到 16/16 显著——方法宽松时一切 \\\\\\\"显著\\\\\\\"；**(Layer 2)** 一阶差分翻转整条 perplexity 通道（8/8→0/8 SIG）而 diversity 通道不变（12/12→12/12）——一个方法论选择决定通道存亡（D017）；**(Layer 3)** TY d_max sensitivity 使结论在 ~0%↔94% spurious rate 间震荡——超参数选择同样致命。方法论贡献不是\\\\\\\"提供正确的检验\\\\\\\"，而是**暴露方法选择敏感性并提出多方法一致性框架**——只有跨方法（cross-corr + 差分 + threshold + permutation Granger）和跨 seed 都一致的关联才可信。这是迭代训练文献中被忽视的方法论陷阱。\\\\n\\\\n## Story Spine (D027: Option B reframe)\\\\n\\\\n- **Problem**: Model collapse 文献中的 lead-lag 分析（\\\\\\\"哪些指标预测崩溃\\\\\\\"）日益增多（Du et al., SIGMA, DCES），但**方法选择对结论的影响未被系统性审视**。Du et al. 非正式观察到 ECE 领先 accuracy，仅限单指标对、1-3 代、无统计检验。现有工作的方法论基础薄弱：无差分检验（不排除伪回归）、无多方法交叉验证、无多重比较校正、无 regime-dependent 分析。\\\\n\\\\n- **Gap**: 没有工作系统性回答：(1) lead-lag 结论在多大程度上取决于分析方法的选择？(2) 同一统计检验在不同 contamination regime 下结论是否稳定？(3) 如何建立可靠的 lead-lag 判定框架？**Scheffer et al. (2009, 2012)** 在生态/气候科学中建立了临界转变早期预警框架，但尚未被 model collapse 文献引入，其方法论严谨性（多方法交叉验证 + regime-dependent analysis）恰好是本领域所缺失的。\\\\n\\\\n- **Our approach**: 以 canary prediction for model collapse 为系统性 case study，暴露 lead-lag 分析中的三个方法论陷阱：(1) 方法选择敏感性——同一数据集上 5 种统计方法从 100% 显著到 0% 显著（Claim 3）；(2) 未差分的伪回归——canary→perplexity 通道在差分后完全消失（Claim 1 case study）；(3) regime-dependent validity——同一检验在不同 α 下结论翻转（Claim 2）。在此基础上提出**多方法一致性共识框架**（multi-method consensus framework）作为解决方案：只有跨方法（cross-corr + 差分 + threshold + permutation Granger）和跨 seed 都一致的关联才可信。\\\\n\\\\n- **Headline result (D037 更新)**: \\\\\\\"In iterative synthetic training with Pythia-410M, naive correlation identifies 100% of canary-collapse metric pairs as significant, while differenced permutation Granger retains only 25% — and this method-dependent gap is compounded by regime dependence: the same test applied to the same metric pair yields 24/24 FDR-significant results at α=1.0, 3/4 at α=0.75, but 0/8 at α≤0.50. We propose a multi-method consensus framework and, applying it, identify token entropy as a robust 1-generation-ahead canary for diversity collapse at α≥0.75.\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n- **Title direction**: \\\\\\\"Method Matters: Pitfalls and a Multi-Method Consensus Framework for Lead-Lag Analysis in Iterative Training Collapse\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n- **文献定位 (保留)**：SIGMA 提供谱监控但无 lead-time 量化；DCES 反应式而非预测式；Du et al. 是最接近的经验前辈但方法论不足；Kalinowski 拓扑 CI 针对表征坍缩非合成数据退化且计算昂贵。Truong & Truong 证明一阶相变下标准早期预警指标失效。理论分析方面：Seddik et al. (2024), Gerstgrasser et al. (2024), Dey et al. (2024) 提供互补理论视角但不涉及方法论敏感性。**我们与 Du et al. 的核心区别**：(1) 多指标家族系统对比；(2) 多方法交叉验证 + d_max sensitivity；(3) regime-dependent mapping；(4) **方法选择敏感性本身是主要贡献**（不再是\\\\\\\"独立贡献\\\\\\\"而是\\\\\\\"核心贡献\\\\\\\"）。\\\\n\\\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\\\n\\\\n| Concern | Response Strategy | Required Experiment Consistent temporal precedence (cross-correlation lag=+1, r=0.80–0.94) is observed across all contamination levels, though formal Granger tests at α≤0.50 do not reach FDR-corrected significance at T=11 (power analysis: §X).\\\"*升级上限：Granger ≠ true causality（Granger 1969），Discussion 一句话声明；双变量模型无法排除未观测混淆因子（详见 Reviewer Concerns 表）。ECE 前向 consensus 与 token_entropy 等效但存在 bidirectional coupling（详见约束 #9）。注意：TY Granger 在 N=11 下不可用于区分真/伪关联（见 Claim 3），核心证据链基于多方法一致性而非单一 TY 背书。**D063 泛化证据**：Table 7 跨 7 conditions（3 architectures × 2 scales × 2 domains）K≥4=5/8-6/8。**Diversity-only subset（排除→ppl pairs）consensus 41/42 = 97.6%**，仅 1.4B s42 的 1 个 diversity pair 未达 K≥4。→ppl pairs 0/14 达 K≥4（结构性失败，非方法缺陷），canary→perplexity 的系统性弱信号作为 explicit finding 报告——diversity collapse 是可预测的下游维度，perplexity 不是。\\n2. **Regime-Dependent Predictability**: 可预测性取决于 collapse 维度和 contamination regime——canary→diversity 在差分后仍 robust（6/6 pair × 4/4 seed），但 canary→perplexity **经差分检验确认为伪回归**（0/4 seed, F≈0, p=0.99），未差分时的\\\"信号\\\"（F=16-36）完全由共享非平稳趋势驱动（D017）。ECE→perplexity 的反向因果同理存疑。**Claim 2 reframing (D022)**: 从\\\"不同 α 下 lead time 不同\\\"升级为\\\"不同 α 下 **detectability** 不同\\\"——α=0.25 差分 Granger 0/8 FDR sig vs α=1.0 的 24/24，同一 pair 的统计可检测性随 contamination level 剧变。**D037 更新**：5-point regime map 完整，detectability 在 α=0.50→0.75 间出现阶跃（0/8→3/8 FDR-sig），α=0.75 consensus 11/16 robust。**Evidence strategy (Reviewer 共识)**：Primary = raw drift magnitude dose-response（distinct_1: -14%@α=0 → -40%@α=0.25 → ~-93%@α=1.0），方法无关、不可争议；Secondary = 差分 Granger FDR sig rate vs α，展示 detectability boundary at α~0.75。**不用 naive_xcorr 做 primary**——与 Claim 3 自相矛盾。Predictability regime map 划定可预测/不可预测边界（\\\"phase diagram\\\" 需 ≥6 个 α 值 + uncertainty interval 才成立，当前 5 个 α 值下用 \\\"regime map\\\"）。\\n3. **Method Selection Sensitivity and Regime-Dependent Validity**（升级自 Methodological Rigor；D022 升级候选：method × regime interaction）: 在典型 generation 数（N≈10）下，lead-lag 结论对分析方法和超参数高度敏感，且**同一方法在不同 α regime 下结论翻转**（diff Granger: α=1.0 24/24 FDR sig → α=0.25 0/8）。升级措辞候选：\\\"Method sensitivity is compounded by regime dependence: the same statistical test applied to the same metric pair can yield significant or null results depending on contamination level\\\"。**三层证据结构**：**(Layer 1)** naive Pearson 找到 16/16 显著——方法宽松时一切 \\\"显著\\\"；**(Layer 2)** 一阶差分翻转整条 perplexity 通道（8/8→0/8 SIG）而 diversity 通道不变（12/12→12/12）——一个方法论选择决定通道存亡（D017）；**(Layer 3)** TY d_max sensitivity 使结论在 ~0%↔94% spurious rate 间震荡——超参数选择同样致命。方法论贡献不是\\\"提供正确的检验\\\"，而是**暴露方法选择敏感性并提出多方法一致性框架**——只有跨方法（cross-corr + 差分 + threshold + permutation Granger）和跨 seed 都一致的关联才可信。这是迭代训练文献中被忽视的方法论陷阱。\\n\\n## Story Spine (D027: Option B reframe)\\n\\n- **Problem**: Model collapse 文献中的 lead-lag 分析（\\\"哪些指标预测崩溃\\\"）日益增多（Du et al., SIGMA, DCES），但**方法选择对结论的影响未被系统性审视**。Du et al. 非正式观察到 ECE 领先 accuracy，仅限单指标对、1-3 代、无统计检验。现有工作的方法论基础薄弱：无差分检验（不排除伪回归）、无多方法交叉验证、无多重比较校正、无 regime-dependent 分析。\\n\\n- **Gap**: 没有工作系统性回答：(1) lead-lag 结论在多大程度上取决于分析方法的选择？(2) 同一统计检验在不同 contamination regime 下结论是否稳定？(3) 如何建立可靠的 lead-lag 判定框架？**Scheffer et al. (2009, 2012)** 在生态/气候科学中建立了临界转变早期预警框架，但尚未被 model collapse 文献引入，其方法论严谨性（多方法交叉验证 + regime-dependent analysis）恰好是本领域所缺失的。\\n\\n- **Our approach**: 以 canary prediction for model collapse 为系统性 case study，暴露 lead-lag 分析中的三个方法论陷阱：(1) 方法选择敏感性——同一数据集上 5 种统计方法从 100% 显著到 0% 显著（Claim 3）；(2) 未差分的伪回归——canary→perplexity 通道在差分后完全消失（Claim 1 case study）；(3) regime-dependent validity——同一检验在不同 α 下结论翻转（Claim 2）。在此基础上提出**多方法一致性共识框架**（multi-method consensus framework）作为解决方案：只有跨方法（cross-corr + 差分 + threshold + permutation Granger）和跨 seed 都一致的关联才可信。\\n\\n- **Headline result (D037 更新)**: \\\"In iterative synthetic training with Pythia-410M, naive correlation identifies 100% of canary-collapse metric pairs as significant, while differenced permutation Granger retains only 25% — and this method-dependent gap is compounded by regime dependence: the same test applied to the same metric pair yields 24/24 FDR-significant results at α=1.0, 3/4 at α=0.75, but 0/8 at α≤0.50. We propose a multi-method consensus framework and, applying it, identify token entropy as a robust 1-generation-ahead canary for diversity collapse at α≥0.75.\\\"\\n\\n- **Title direction**: \\\"Method Matters: Pitfalls and a Multi-Method Consensus Framework for Lead-Lag Analysis in Iterative Training Collapse\\\"\\n\\n- **文献定位 (保留)**：SIGMA 提供谱监控但无 lead-time 量化；DCES 反应式而非预测式；Du et al. 是最接近的经验前辈但方法论不足；Kalinowski 拓扑 CI 针对表征坍缩非合成数据退化且计算昂贵。Truong & Truong 证明一阶相变下标准早期预警指标失效。理论分析方面：Seddik et al. (2024), Gerstgrasser et al. (2024), Dey et al. (2024) 提供互补理论视角但不涉及方法论敏感性。**我们与 Du et al. 的核心区别**：(1) 多指标家族系统对比；(2) 多方法交叉验证 + d_max sensitivity；(3) regime-dependent mapping；(4) **方法选择敏感性本身是主要贡献**（不再是\\\"独立贡献\\\"而是\\\"核心贡献\\\"）。\\n\\n## Anticipated ECE secondary 原因升级为 bidirectionality**\n- 旧叙事：ECE 是 secondary canary 因为 TY Type II error\n- 新叙事：ECE 是 secondary canary 因为 **genuine bidirectional coupling**\n- 证据：token_entropy reverse = **0/28** across 7 conditions（完美单向因果）；ECE reverse = **13/28 significant, 5/7 conditions 有 ≥1 reverse pair**，全部为 distinct→ECE 方向\n- 论文措辞参考：\"While both canaries achieve comparable forward consensus (PG_fwd=6/8), token_entropy shows no reverse significance (0/28), consistent with unidirectional causal precedence; ECE exhibits significant reverse coupling in 5/7 conditions (13/28 pairs), suggesting bidirectional feedback.\"\n- 这同时服务 Claim 3：单看前向 sig 两者等价，加入反向检验后分离——分析维度选择改变结论\n\n**2. →ppl pairs 0/14 升级为 explicit finding**\n- 旧叙事：canary→perplexity 弱/不显著（被当作 limitation）\n- 新叙事：canary→perplexity 0/14 达 K≥4 = **结构性失败**，作为 **explicit finding 报告**——diversity collapse 是可预测的下游维度，perplexity 不是\n- 这不是方法缺陷（差分后确认为伪回归 D017），而是揭示了 predictability 的结构性边界\n\n**3. diversity-only subset consensus 41/42 = 97.6%**\n- 排除 →ppl pairs 后，diversity 相关 pairs 的 K≥4 consensus = 41/42 = 97.6%，仅 1.4B s42 的 1 个 diversity pair 未达 K≥4\n- 这是论文的 headline 数字之一，应在 abstract、introduction contributions、experiments 正文中出现\n\n### 需要编辑的文件\n\n论文文件在：`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/_project/paper/`\n\n需要检查并可能修改的文件：\n1. **abstract.tex** — 加入 diversity-only 41/42 或 97.6%，确保 ECE 叙事对齐\n2. **introduction.tex** — contributions 列表和叙事中反映 diversity-only consensus + →ppl finding + ECE bidirectionality\n3. **experiments.tex** — Table 7 叙事部分（Architecture/Scale/Domain 分析已被上一个 worker 更新，但 D063 特定的 ECE bidirectionality 叙事、→ppl explicit finding、diversity-only 41/42 可能未写入），特别关注：\n   - 是否有一段专门讨论 ECE vs token_entropy 的 reverse profile\n   - 是否有 →ppl 0/14 的显式报告\n   - 是否有 diversity-only 41/42 的数字\n4. **discussion.tex** — ECE bidirectionality 讨论、→ppl structural finding\n5. **conclusion.tex** — ECE 和 diversity-only 数字\n\n### 写作规范（必须遵守）\n\n- **禁止 em-dash（—）和解释性 en-dash（–）**，用逗号、括号或拆分句子替代\n- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**（如 \"..., demonstrating the...\"）\n- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**\n- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"a myriad of\"\n- 段落 5-8 句是正常的，不要过度分段\n- 每个数字必须服务于叙事推进，不要堆砌\n- 缩写首次出现必须定义\n\n### 验证步骤\n\n完成修改后：\n1. `grep \"—\" *.tex` 和 `grep \" – \" *.tex` 确认无解释性 dash\n2. `grep -i \"preliminary\" *.tex` 确认无 preliminary\n3. `grep -i \"TBD\\|TODO\\|pending\" *.tex` 确认无遗留\n4. 检查 D063 三个叙事变更是否都已落地：ECE bidirectionality、→ppl explicit finding、diversity-only 41/42\n\n### 输出\n\n完成后列出每个文件的具体修改清单（行号 + old→new）。",

statistical methods vary dramatically across conditions (TY: 0/8 to 6/8), yet the multi-method consensus threshold (K≥4) remains remarkably stable at 5/8-6/8, absorbing per-method variance.\"\n\n### 数据（Table 7, n_perm=5000 confirmed）\n\n7 conditions × 6 methods + consensus:\n\n| Condition | Naive | Diff | Thresh | PG_fwd | TY | K≥4 |\n|-----------|-------|------|--------|--------|-----|-----|\n| 0/8-3/8），但本图聚焦前向（forward）方法。\n\n### 图表设计建议\n\n**方案 A（推荐）：Dot-strip + consensus band**\n- X 轴：7 conditions\n- Y 轴：sig rate (fraction, 0-1)\n- 每个 condition 上用不同颜色/形状的点表示 5 个方法的 sig rate（散开避免重叠，jitter）\n- K≥4 consensus 用粗线或带状区域（shaded band）叠加\n- 关键视觉效果：点散布很广（尤其 TY），但共识带很窄\n\n**方案 B：Heatmap + sidebar**\n- 主体：method × condition heatmap（颜色编码 sig rate）\n- 右侧 sidebar：K≥4 consensus ```text
1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Granger precedence is not causation.}
4	Granger causality tests whether past values of one series improve prediction of another beyond its own history; it does not establish true causation~\cite{granger1969investigating,shojaie2022granger}.
5	Bivariate Granger tests cannot exclude confounders: an unobserved process---such as a latent distributional shift in the training data---could independently drive both token entropy and diversity metrics, producing spurious lead-lag associations even when no direct relationship exists.
6	Our multi-method consensus framework inherits this limitation in full.
7	Throughout this paper, ``Granger precedence'' denotes predictive improvement in a time-series forecasting sense, not a causal mechanism.
8	This distinction is why we scope our central claim to ``token entropy Granger-precedes diversity collapse'' rather than asserting that token entropy \emph{causes} diversity collapse.
9	
10	\paragraph{Calibration scope and generalization.}
11	All FPR and FNR calibration results reported in this work are specific to our experimental setup: Pythia-410M~\cite{biderman2023pythia}, a WikiText-103 subset, $T{=}10$ generation cycles, and five contamination levels ($\alpha \in \{0.0, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.0\}$).
12	Generalization to other model architectures, datasets, or training protocols requires independent validation.
13	The consensus framework's structure---multi-method agreement combined with multi-seed replication---is intended to be applicable beyond this specific setting, but the operating thresholds ($K{\geq}4/5$ methods, $S{\geq}3/4$ seeds) are empirically calibrated and may require re-tuning under different conditions.
14	For instance, architectures with different tokenizer granularity or training dynamics could shift the balance between false positive and false negative rates at these thresholds.
15	Our ongoing GPT-2-medium experiments represent a first step toward cross-architecture validation, though results are not yet available.
16	
17	\paragraph{FPR/FNR calibration asymmetry.}
18	The FPR and FNR estimates in our calibration analysis differ in their coverage of the seed dimension.
19	FPR is validated on the method dimension only, using a single seed at the null-control condition ($\alpha{=}0.0$).
20	FNR is validated on the joint method${\times}$seed dimension, aggregating across four seeds at full contamination ($\alpha{=}1.0$).
21	This asymmetry means our FPR estimate of 0\% may be optimistic: if seed-level variance in false positive rates is non-negligible, the true FPR under the consensus rule could exceed zero.
22	Full joint calibration---requiring multi-seed null-control experiments at $\alpha{=}0.0$---remains an open direction for future work.
23	
24	\paragraph{Short time-series constraint.}
25	With $T{=}10$ generation cycles (11 data points per series), our time-series analyses operate near the lower bound of what standard methods require.
26	This series length is typical for iterative training studies, where each generation involves a complete training run, but it severely limits statistical power.
27	The Toda--Yamamoto procedure's elevated FNR (33.3\%, missing 2 of 6 directional pairs) is attributable to insufficient residual degrees of freedom at this series length, not to a fundamental deficiency in the method itself.
28	This finding aligns with \citet{boettiger2012limits}, who demonstrated that early-warning signals have limited detection power in short time series.
29	Longer generation chains would improve power for all methods but impose substantial computational cost; this trade-off between statistical power and compute budget is inherent to iterative training research and cannot be resolved by methodological improvements alone.
30	However, observation window sensitivity analysis provides reassurance: the $4/5$ consensus classification stabilizes at $T{\geq}5$, well below our observation window of $T{=}10$, with identical results from $T{=}5$ through $T{=}10$.
31	The Toda--Yamamoto procedure produces valid test statistics at all window lengths ($T \in \{5, \ldots, 10\}$), but with unstable sensitivity---3/6 to 6/6 pairs reach significance depending on window length---confirming that its elevated FNR reflects limited residual degrees of freedom rather than absence of signal.
32	
33	\paragraph{Diversity--calibration dissociation.}
34	Diversity metrics and calibration metrics respond to synthetic contamination in qualitatively opposite directions across the $\alpha$ spectrum.
35	Diversity collapse (distinct-$n$ decline) is most severe at low contamination ($\alpha{=}0.25$: distinct-3 declines by 59.9\%) and attenuates at higher levels ($\alpha{=}1.0$: $-30.5\%$), while calibration degradation (ECE) increases monotonically with $\alpha$ ($+51.7\%$ at $\alpha{=}0.0$ to $+3782\%$ at $\alpha{=}1.0$).
36	This dissociation underscores the need for multi-method consensus: the regime in which a metric degrades most severely ($\alpha{=}0.25$ for diversity) does not coincide with the regime in which its temporal lead is most detectable ($\alpha{\geq}0.75$), and a single metric family cannot capture the full picture of iterative training degradation.
37	
38	\paragraph{Limitations.}
39	Several limitations constrain the generalizability of our findings.
40	First, our primary experiments use Pythia-410M; preliminary cross-architecture validation with GPT-2-medium uses batch size~32 (versus~4 for Pythia) for GPU efficiency, exposing 8$\times$ more data per training step, which may accelerate collapse dynamics without altering the qualitative pattern (token entropy leading diversity decline is observed in both architectures).
41	Second, training data is drawn from a single domain (a WikiText-103 subset), and domain-specific distributional properties may influence the coupling between canary and downstream metrics.
42	Third, we lack an intervention experiment: we observe that token entropy Granger-precedes diversity collapse at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (with preliminary single-seed evidence at $\alpha{=}0.75$) but do not test whether acting on canary signals can prevent or delay collapse onset.
43	Fourth, our experiments use replace-only data accumulation; accumulate-mode training~\cite{dey2024breaking,kazdan2025collapse} may exhibit qualitatively different canary dynamics.
44	Finally, our canary claims are scoped to distinct-$n$ diversity metrics~\cite{li2016diversity}---not to model collapse broadly~\cite{schaeffer2025position}---as the perplexity channel proved spurious ($F{\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$ after differencing).
45	This scoping is theoretically motivated: \citet{cao2025entropycal} prove a fundamental calibration-diversity tradeoff in language models---all current calibration methods reduce entropy at the cost of diversity---suggesting that calibration and diversity are coupled but not identical, which may explain why token entropy can Granger-precede diversity collapse without leading perplexity.
46	
47	\paragraph{Future work.}
48	Several directions extend this work.
49	Multivariate VAR models would enable testing for confounders and multi-directional Granger relationships beyond our current bivariate framework.
50	Cross-architecture validation is needed: our GPT-2-medium experiments are in progress, and evaluation on larger-scale models would further test generalizability.
51	Online monitoring---integrating canary metrics into training pipelines for real-time collapse detection---is a natural applied extension.
52	Accumulate-mode experiments~\cite{dey2024breaking} would test whether canary dynamics differ from the replace-mode setting studied here.
53	Finally, connecting the empirically observed detectability boundary at $\alpha{\approx}0.50$--$0.75$ to theoretical phase transition analysis~\cite{truong2025entropy} could yield a principled explanation for why canary signals emerge at this contamination regime rather than at lower contamination levels.
```

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We study lead-lag analysis pitfalls in iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M \cite{biderman2023pythia}, a 410M-parameter autoregressive language model.
8	Pythia provides a controlled setting: small enough for multi-seed experiments across five contamination levels, yet large enough to exhibit meaningful generation-over-generation degradation.
9	Training data is drawn from WikiText-103 \cite{merity2017pointer}, using 5{,}000-sample subsets per generation.
10	
11	Our protocol follows the full-replacement paradigm of \citet{shumailov2024curse}.
12	Each generation $t$ proceeds in three steps: (1)~fine-tune the generation-$(t{-}1)$ model for 5{,}000 steps on a mixture of $\alpha$ fraction synthetic data and $(1{-}\alpha)$ fraction real data; (2)~generate 5{,}000 synthetic samples from the fine-tuned model; (3)~fully replace the synthetic pool.
13	We run $T{=}10$ generations (11 time points including the generation-0 baseline) across contamination levels $\alpha \in \{0.00, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00\}$.
14	The $\alpha{=}1.0$ condition uses four random seeds $\{42, 43, 44, 45\}$; other conditions use seed~42, with multi-seed replication in progress.
15	
16	We track two \emph{canary metrics}---cheap-to-compute statistics hypothesized to precede downstream degradation, inspired by early-warning signals in dynamical systems \cite{scheffer2009early}---and three \emph{downstream metrics}.
17	Canary metrics are token entropy (next-token prediction entropy averaged over held-out samples) and expected calibration error (ECE).
18	Downstream metrics are distinct-1 and distinct-2 ($n$-gram diversity \cite{li2016diversity}) and perplexity.
19	We additionally evaluate effective rank and Self-BLEU as candidate canaries (\S\ref{sec:case_study}).
20	Results for distinct-3 are reported in Appendix~\ref{app:distinct3}.
21	
22	All hypothesis tests apply Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR correction at $q{=}0.05$.
23	Permutation Granger causality tests use 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series \cite{shojaie2022granger}.
24	Cross-correlation analysis uses a maximum lag of 5 generations.
25	Table~\ref{tab:drift_summary} summarizes raw metric drift from generation~0 to generation~10 across all five contamination levels.
26	
27	\begin{table}[t]
28	\centering
29	\small
30	\caption{Metric drift (\%, generation~0$\to$10) across contamination levels. $\alpha{=}1.0$: 4-seed mean${\pm}$std; other levels: single seed (seed=42). Non-monotonic distinct-1 pattern across $\alpha \in \{0.25, 0.50\}$ is observed at seed=42, pending multi-seed confirmation.}
31	\label{tab:drift_summary}
32	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
33	\toprule
34	$\alpha$ & Entropy & ECE & Distinct-1 & Distinct-2 & Perplexity \\
35	\midrule
36	0.00 & $-5.1$ & $+51.7$ & $-13.8$ & $-9.8$ & $-21.7$ \\
37	0.25 & $-4.1$ & $+79.7$ & $-40.3$ & $-57.1$ & $-17.2$ \\
38	0.50 & $-18.6$ & $+842.8$ & $-37.3$ & $-51.5$ & $+21.1$ \\
39	0.75 & $-27.7$ & $+1809.5$ & $-32.7$ & $-41.3$ & $+182.1$ \\
40	1.00 & $-60.8{\pm}1.2$ & $+3782{\pm}30$ & $-27.8{\pm}1.6$ & $-34.0{\pm}3.3$ & $+3673{\pm}45$ \\
41	\bottomrule
42	\end{tabular}
43	\end{table}
44	
45	
46	\subsection{Method Sensitivity Analysis}
47	\label{sec:method_sensitivity}
48	
49	We open with Claim~3---method sensitivity---because it conditions the interpretation of all subsequent results.
50	If different statistical methods yield contradictory conclusions on the same data, any single-method analysis is unreliable by construction.
51	
52	\paragraph{Five-method ablation.}
53	We apply five lead-lag detection methods to the $\alpha{=}1.0$ condition (4~seeds, 16 canary$\to$downstream pairs).
54	The fraction of pairs reaching BH-corrected FDR significance varies dramatically by method: naive cross-correlation \textbf{16/16} (100\%), differenced cross-correlation \textbf{14/16} (87.5\%), threshold onset detection \textbf{9/16} (56.3\%), permutation Granger causality \textbf{6/16} (37.5\%), and Toda--Yamamoto Granger \textbf{4/16} (25.0\%).
55	Method choice alone swings significance from \textbf{100\% to 25\%}.
56	The spurious-signal rate---the fraction of pairs detected by the most liberal method but not confirmed by the most conservative---is \textbf{75\%} across all five methods and \textbf{62.5\%} when excluding Toda--Yamamoto, whose low statistical power at $T{=}10$ inflates this rate (\S\ref{sec:consensus_validation}).
57	Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison} reports significance rates restricted to 8 validated canary$\to$downstream pairs (excluding failed candidates); the 16-pair rates above include effective rank and Self-BLEU to capture the full method-sensitivity landscape.
58	
59	\paragraph{First-differencing diagnostic.}
60	A single preprocessing decision can eliminate an entire analysis channel.
61	For canary$\to$diversity pairs at $\alpha{=}1.0$, all pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing (24/24$\to$24/24), confirming genuine temporal precedence rather than shared-trend confounding.
62	For canary$\to$perplexity, however, undifferenced tests find near-universal significance ($F{=}16$--$36$) while differenced tests find \textbf{zero} significant pairs ($F{\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$; Figure~\ref{fig:differencing_dmax}a).
63	The apparent canary$\to$perplexity relationship is entirely an artifact of co-trending.
64	
65	\paragraph{Augmentation order sensitivity.}
66	Toda--Yamamoto Granger tests show extreme sensitivity to the augmentation order $d_{\max}$ (Figure~\ref{fig:differencing_dmax}b).
67	At $d_{\max}{=}0$ (most permissive), approximately 6\% of pairs are rejected; at adaptive $d_{\max}$ determined by ADF unit-root tests, 75\%; at $d_{\max}{=}1$ (most conservative), \textbf{94\%}.
68	This single hyperparameter swings conclusions from near-universal significance to near-universal non-significance.
69	
70	\paragraph{Inter-method agreement.}
71	Cohen's $\kappa$, averaged over all method pairs, is \textbf{0.185} (poor agreement); this value is consistent across contamination levels ($\alpha{=}0.0$ through $\alpha{=}1.0$), indicating that method disagreement is a structural property of the analysis rather than a regime-specific artifact.
72	Cross-architecture validation on GPT-2-medium (12 canary$\to$downstream pairs) yields Fleiss' $\kappa{=}0.091$, also in the ``slight agreement'' range, confirming that low inter-method agreement is not architecture-specific.
73	Restricting to three informative methods (excluding naive cross-correlation and Toda--Yamamoto, which anchor the permissive and conservative extremes respectively) raises $\kappa$ to 0.667 (substantial agreement), indicating that the low full-panel $\kappa$ is driven by the deliberate inclusion of methods spanning the entire permissive-to-conservative spectrum rather than by fundamental incoherence among methods.
74	Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison} extends the analysis across contamination levels.
75	At $\alpha{=}0.0$ (no synthetic data, serving as null control), naive cross-correlation finds \textbf{100\%} of pairs significant---a textbook spurious-regression outcome in trending time series.
76	Permutation Granger and Toda--Yamamoto correctly find 0\%.
77	At $\alpha{=}0.50$, correlation-based methods (naive, differenced, threshold) unanimously find significance while causal methods unanimously find non-significance (Figure~\ref{fig:consensus_heatmap}), the most extreme method$\times$regime divergence in our experiments.
78	
79	\paragraph{Method$\times$regime interaction.}
80	Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison} reveals that the correlation-versus-causal divergence is not uniform across contamination levels.
81	At $\alpha{=}0.25$, differenced cross-correlation drops to 25\% and threshold onset to 37.5\%, while naive cross-correlation remains at 100\%---demonstrating that even partial detrending removes marginal associations at low contamination.
82	The graded response across methods, combined with the block structure visible in Figure~\ref{fig:consensus_heatmap}, motivates the regime-dependent analysis in \S\ref{sec:regime}.
83	
84	\begin{table}[t]
85	\centering
86	\small
87	\caption{FDR significance rates (\%) for five lead-lag methods across contamination levels. Each cell reports the fraction of 8 primary canary$\to$downstream pairs (token entropy and ECE $\times$ 4 downstream metrics) reaching BH-corrected significance ($q{=}0.05$); failed candidates (effective rank, Self-BLEU) excluded per \S\ref{sec:case_study}. $\alpha{=}0.75$ is analyzed separately as single-seed data (\S\ref{sec:regime}). FPR$_\text{null}$: false positive rate at the $\alpha{=}0.0$ null control.}
88	\label{tab:method_comparison}
89	\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}
90	\toprule
91	Method & $\alpha{=}0.0$ & $\alpha{=}0.25$ & $\alpha{=}0.50$ & $\alpha{=}0.75$ & $\alpha{=}1.0$ & FPR$_\text{null}$ \\
92	\midrule
93	Naive XCorr     & 100   & 100  & 100  & ---  & 100   & 100  \\
94	Diff.\ XCorr   & 37.5  & 25   & 100  & ---  & 100   & 37.5 \\
95	Threshold       & 62.5  & 37.5 & 100  & ---  & 100   & 62.5 \\
96	Perm.\ Granger  & 0     & 0    & 0    & ---  & 75    & 0    \\
97	Toda--Yamamoto  & 0     & 37.5 & 0    & ---  & 59.4  & 0    \\
98	\bottomrule
99	\end{tabular}
100	\end{table}
101	
102	
103	\subsection{Regime-Dependent Detectability}
104	\label{sec:regime}
105	
106	Beyond method choice, the contamination level~$\alpha$ determines whether a temporal lead exists to detect.
107	We present regime effects at two levels: raw metric drift (method-independent) and Granger-causal detectability.
108	
109	\paragraph{Raw drift.}
110	Table~\ref{tab:drift_summary} shows that diversity collapse occurs at \emph{all} contamination levels, not only under full synthetic replacement.
111	Distinct-1 declines by 13.8\% even at $\alpha{=}0.0$ (attributable to fine-tuning on repeated real-data subsets), and more steeply at intermediate contamination: $-40.3\%$ ($\alpha{=}0.25$), $-37.3\%$ ($\alpha{=}0.50$), $-32.7\%$ ($\alpha{=}0.75$), and $-27.8{\pm}1.6\%$ ($\alpha{=}1.0$).
112	The non-monotonic pattern---diversity declining \emph{more} at moderate~$\alpha$ than at extreme~$\alpha$---is observed at seed=42 and requires multi-seed confirmation before drawing conclusions about the relationship between contamination level and collapse severity.
113	
114	\paragraph{Granger-causal detectability.}
115	Despite ubiquitous drift, causal detectability is sharply regime-dependent (Figure~\ref{fig:regime_map}).
116	Permutation Granger tests (first-differenced, BH-corrected) find 0/8 pairs FDR-significant at $\alpha{=}0.00$, 0/8 at $\alpha{=}0.25$, 0/8 at $\alpha{=}0.50$, and \textbf{6/8} at $\alpha{=}1.0$ in the cross-$\alpha$ pooled analysis.
117	At $\alpha{=}0.75$ (single seed, seed=42), 3/8 pairs reach FDR significance; this result requires multi-seed replication before serving as primary evidence.
118	Within-$\alpha$ multi-seed analysis at $\alpha{=}1.0$ yields \textbf{24/24} canary$\to$diversity pairs FDR-significant across all four seeds, with 0/24 in the reverse direction, confirming temporal asymmetry.
119	The detectability boundary---the regime below which canary metrics no longer develop sufficient temporal lead for causal inference---lies at $\alpha \approx 0.50$--$0.75$, consistent with theoretical expectations about signal-to-noise limits in short time series \cite{boettiger2012limits}.
120	This boundary is sharp rather than gradual: method-level consensus ($K{\geq}4/5$) identifies 0--1 of 16 candidate pairs as robust at $\alpha{\leq}0.50$ but 11/16 at $\alpha{=}0.75$ (single seed, pre-pipeline filtering; the stricter $K{\geq}4/5$, $S{\geq}3/4$ criterion at $\alpha{=}1.0$ retains 6/16 after excluding failed candidates and spurious channels per \S\ref{sec:case_study}).
121	The lower count at $\alpha{=}1.0$ despite stronger contamination reflects the stricter validation standard: multi-seed replication ($S{\geq}3/4$) filters seed-specific artifacts, and pipeline filtering removes the perplexity channel (spurious per \S\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}) and failed canary candidates (effective rank, Self-BLEU), both of which contribute to the $\alpha{=}0.75$ single-seed count.
122	
123	\paragraph{Synchronous collapse at $\alpha{=}0.50$.}
124	The $\alpha{=}0.50$ regime provides particularly informative evidence.
125	Correlation-based methods unanimously find significance (Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison}) while causal methods unanimously find non-significance.
126	This extreme method$\times$regime interaction arises because canary and downstream metrics collapse \emph{synchronously} at moderate contamination, preserving high correlation but eliminating the temporal lead required for Granger-causal inference.
127	We highlight this as a \emph{positive finding}: it delineates the regime boundary where naive analysis is most dangerous---high correlation coexists with zero causal precedence, maximizing the risk of spurious conclusions.
128	Figure~\ref{fig:consensus_heatmap} visualizes this interaction across all method$\times\alpha$ combinations, revealing a clear block structure that separates correlation-based methods from causal methods as a function of regime.
129	
130	
131	\subsection{Case Study: Canary Prediction of Diversity Collapse}
132	\label{sec:case_study}
133	
134	Having established method sensitivity (Claim~3) and regime dependence (Claim~2), we present the conditions under which canary prediction succeeds (Claim~1).
135	We scope this claim precisely: canary metrics predict \emph{diversity collapse} (declining distinct-$n$), not model collapse broadly.
136	
137	\paragraph{Full synthetic replacement ($\alpha{=}1.0$, 4 seeds).}
138	Token entropy Granger-causes distinct-1 and distinct-2 with a consistent one-generation lead.
139	Cross-correlation peaks at lag$={+}1$ with $|r|{>}0.99$ (cross-seed standard deviation: 0.0).
140	Figure~\ref{fig:metric_trajectory} illustrates the one-generation offset between token entropy decline and distinct-1 decline, visible across all four seeds.
141	Permutation Granger tests (first-differenced) confirm all canary$\to$diversity pairs as FDR-significant across all four seeds, including distinct-3 (Appendix~\ref{app:distinct3}).
142	Reverse-direction tests (downstream$\to$canary) find \textbf{0/24} FDR-significant (all $p{>}0.49$), confirming directional asymmetry.
143	
144	\paragraph{High contamination ($\alpha{=}0.75$, single seed).}
145	At $\alpha{=}0.75$ (seed=42), three of four forward-direction pairs reach FDR significance: ECE$\to$distinct-1 ($p_\text{fdr}{=}0.046$), ECE$\to$distinct-2 ($p_\text{fdr}{=}0.046$), and token entropy$\to$distinct-2 ($p_\text{fdr}{=}0.048$).
146	Token entropy$\to$distinct-1 is borderline ($p_\text{fdr}{=}0.054$).
147	All four reverse directions are non-significant ($p{>}0.49$).
148	These single-seed results require multi-seed confirmation before serving as primary evidence.
149	
150	\paragraph{ECE as a conditional secondary canary.}
151	ECE performs equivalently to token entropy across four of five methods (naive, differenced, threshold, permutation Granger).
152	Its classification as ``secondary'' is driven entirely by Toda--Yamamoto Type~II errors at $T{=}10$, where bootstrap power analysis yields estimated power of 0.05--0.20 depending on $d_{\max}$.
153	Resolving ECE's true canary status requires longer time series.
154	
155	\paragraph{Failed canary candidates.}
156	Not all metrics serve as canaries.
157	Effective rank and Self-BLEU show no detectable lead-lag relationship with any downstream metric under any method or contamination level, and are excluded from the consensus framework.
158	
159	\paragraph{Consensus filtering.}
160	Of 16 canary$\to$downstream pairs tested at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (4 candidate canaries $\times$ 4 downstream metrics), \textbf{6 survive} the full consensus pipeline ($K{\geq}4/5$ methods, $S{\geq}3/4$ seeds): token entropy and ECE each predicting distinct-1 and distinct-2 (4 pairs; distinct-3 results in Appendix~\ref{app:distinct3} bring the total to~6).
161	The 10 eliminated pairs comprise all canary$\to$perplexity relationships (identified as spurious by the first-differencing diagnostic) and all effective-rank and Self-BLEU pairs (failed canaries).
162	The consensus framework reduces the naive 100\% significance rate to the 37.5\% rate supported by the most conservative individual methods (Figure~\ref{fig:consensus_workflow}).
163	
164	
165	\subsection{Consensus Framework Validation}
166	\label{sec:consensus_validation}
167	
168	We validate the consensus framework's calibration using built-in experimental controls.
169	
170	\paragraph{False positive rate.}
171	At $\alpha{=}0.0$, no synthetic data is injected; any detected lead-lag association is a false positive.
172	Individual-method FPR ranges from \textbf{100\%} (naive cross-correlation: 8/8 pairs) through 62.5\% (threshold) and 37.5\% (differenced) down to \textbf{0\%} (permutation Granger and Toda--Yamamoto).
173	The consensus framework at $K{\geq}4/5$ achieves \textbf{FPR${=}$0\%}, matching the best individual methods while providing robustness against miscalibration of any single method (Table~\ref{tab:fpr_fnr}).
174	
175	\paragraph{False negative rate.}
176	At $\alpha{=}1.0$, we define 6 ground-truth pairs (token entropy and ECE $\to$ distinct-1/2/3), validated independently by the first-differencing diagnostic: all 6 pairs retain FDR significance after differencing, ruling out shared-trend confounding.
177	The consensus framework ($K{\geq}4/5$, $S{\geq}3/4$) recovers all~6 (\textbf{FNR${=}$0\%}).
178	Toda--Yamamoto alone yields FNR${=}$33.3\% (2/6), missing ECE$\to$distinct-2 and ECE$\to$distinct-3 due to Type~II errors at $T{=}10$.
179	We note a design asymmetry: FPR is validated on method-only data (single seed at $\alpha{=}0.0$), while FNR uses method$\times$seed joint data (4~seeds at $\alpha{=}1.0$).
180	Full joint calibration---multi-seed null-control experiments---remains future work.
181	Table~\ref{tab:fpr_fnr} reports the calibration results.
182	
183	\begin{table}[t]
184	\centering
185	\small
186	\caption{False positive and false negative rates for individual methods and the consensus framework. FPR: $\alpha{=}0.0$ null control (single seed). FNR: $\alpha{=}1.0$ against 6 ground-truth pairs independently validated by first-differencing. Note dimension asymmetry: FPR is method-only; FNR is method$\times$seed joint (\S\ref{sec:consensus_validation}).}
187	\label{tab:fpr_fnr}
188	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
189	\toprule
190	Criterion & FPR ($\alpha{=}0.0$) & FNR ($\alpha{=}1.0$) \\
191	\midrule
192	Naive XCorr              & 100\%  & 0\%  \\
193	Differenced XCorr        & 37.5\% & 0\%  \\
194	Threshold Onset          & 62.5\% & 0\%  \\
195	Permutation Granger      & 0\%    & 0\%  \\
196	Toda--Yamamoto           & 0\%    & 33.3\% \\
197	\midrule
198	Consensus ($K{\geq}4/5$)              & 0\%    & 0\%  \\
199	Consensus ($K{\geq}4/5$, $S{\geq}3/4$) & 0\%    & 0\%  \\
200	\bottomrule
201	\end{tabular}
202	\end{table}
```

      "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe study lead-lag analysis pitfalls in iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M \\cite{biderman2023pythia}, a 410M-parameter autoregressive language model.\nPythia provides a controlled setting: small enough for multi-seed experiments across five contamination levels, yet large enough to exhibit meaningful generation-over-generation degradation.\nTraining data is drawn from WikiText-103 \\cite{merity2017pointer}, using 5{,}000-sample subsets per generation.\n\nOur protocol follows the full-replacement paradigm of \\citet{shumailov2024curse}.\nEach generation $t$ proceeds in three steps: (1)~fine-tune the generation-$(t{-}1)$ model for 5{,}000 steps on a mixture of $\\alpha$ fraction synthetic data and $(1{-}\\alpha)$ fraction real data; (2)~generate 5{,}000 synthetic samples from the fine-tuned model; (3)~fully replace the synthetic pool.\nWe run $T{=}10$ generations (11 time points including the generation-0 baseline) across contamination levels $\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00\\}$.\nThe $\\alpha{=}1.0$ condition uses four random seeds $\\{42, 43, 44, 45\\}$; other conditions use seed~42, with multi-seed replication in progress.\n\nWe track two \\emph{canary metrics}---cheap-to-compute statistics hypothesized to precede downstream degradation, inspired by early-warning signals in dynamical systems \\cite{scheffer2009early}---and three \\emph{downstream metrics}.\nCanary metrics are token entropy (next-token prediction entropy averaged over held-out samples) and expected calibration error (ECE).\nDownstream metrics are distinct-1 and distinct-2 ($n$-gram diversity \\cite{li2016diversity}) and perplexity.\nWe additionally evaluate effective rank and Self-BLEU as candidate canaries (\\S\\ref{sec:case_study}).\nResults for distinct-3 are reported in Appendix~\\ref{app:distinct3}.\n\nAll hypothesis tests apply Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR correction at $q{=}0.05$.\nPermutation Granger causality tests use 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series \\cite{shojaie2022granger}.\nCross-correlation analysis uses a maximum lag of 5 generations.\nTable~\\ref{tab:drift_summary} summarizes raw metric drift from generation~0 to generation~10 across all five contamination levels.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Metric drift (\\%, generation~0$\\to$10) across contamination levels. $\\alpha{=}1.0$: 4-seed mean${\\pm}$std; Analysis}\n\\label{sec:method_sensitivity}\n\nWe open with Claim~3---method sensitivity---because it conditions the interpretation of all subsequent results.\nIf different statistical methods yield contradictory conclusions on the same data, any single-method analysis is unreliable by construction.\n\n\\paragraph{Five-method ablation.}\nWe apply five lead-lag detection methods to the $\\alpha{=}1.0$ condition (4~seeds, 16 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).\nThe fraction of pairs reaching BH-corrected FDR significance varies dramatically by method: naive cross-correlation \\textbf{16/16} (100\\%), differenced cross-correlation \\textbf{14/16} (87.5\\%), threshold onset detection \\textbf{9/16} (56.3\\%), permutation Granger causality \\textbf{6/16} (37.5\\%), and Toda--Yamamoto Granger \\textbf{4/16} (25.0\\%).\nMethod choice alone swings significance from \\textbf{100\\% to 25\\%}.\nThe spurious-signal rate---the fraction of pairs detected by the most liberal method but not confirmed by the most conservative---is \\textbf{75\\%} across all five methods and \\textbf{62.5\\%} when excluding Toda--Yamamoto, whose low statistical power at $T{=}10$ inflates this rate (\\S\\ref{sec:consensus_validation}).\nTable~\\ref{tab:method_comparison} reports significance rates restricted to 8 validated canary$\\to$downstream pairs (excluding failed candidates); the 16-pair rates above include effective rank and Self-BLEU to capture the full method-sensitivity landscape.\n\n\\paragraph{First-differencing diagnostic.}\nA single preprocessing decision can eliminate an entire analysis channel.\nFor canary$\\to$diversity pairs at $\\alpha{=}1.0$, all pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing (24/24$\\to$24/24), confirming genuine temporal precedence rather than shared-trend confounding.\nFor canary$\\to$perplexity, however, undifferenced tests find near-universal significance ($F{=}16$--$36$) while differenced tests find \\textbf{zero} significant pairs ($F{\\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$; Figure~\\ref{fig:differencing_dmax}a).\nThe apparent canary$\\to$perplexity relationship is entirely an artifact of co-trending.\n\n\\paragraph{Augmentation order sensitivity.}\nToda--Yamamoto Granger tests show extreme sensitivity to the augmentation order $d_{\\max}$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:differencing_dmax}b).\nAt $d_{\\max}{=}0$ (most permissive), approximately 6\\% of pairs are rejected; at adaptive $d_{\\max}$ determined by ADF unit-root tests, 75\\%; at $d_{\\max}{=}1$ (most conservative), \\textbf{94\\%}.\nThis single hyperparameter swings conclusions from near-universal significance to near-universal non-significance.\n\n\\paragraph{Inter-method agreement.}\nCohen's $\\kappa$, averaged over all method pairs, is \\textbf{0.185} (poor agreement); this value is consistent across contamination levels ($\\alpha{=}0.0$ through $\\alpha{=}1.0$), indicating that method disagreement is a structural property of the analysis rather than a regime-specific artifact.\nCross-architecture validation on GPT-2-medium (12 canary$\\to$downstream pairs) yields Fleiss' $\\kappa{=}0.091$, also in the ``slight agreement'' range, confirming that low inter-method agreement is not architecture-specific.\nRestricting to three informative methods (excluding naive cross-correlation and Toda--Yamamoto, which anchor the permissive and conservative extremes respectively) raises $\\kappa$ to 0.667 (substantial agreement), indicating that the low full-panel $\\kappa$ is driven by the deliberate inclusion of methods spanning the entire permissive-to-conservative spectrum rather than by fundamental incoherence among methods.\nTable~\\ref{tab:method_comparison} extends the analysis 120	\paragraph{Generalization design.}
121	% WHY generalization matters (reviewer pre-emption)
122	The method sensitivity, regime dependence, and consensus results above
123	are calibrated on a single model family and dataset (Pythia-410M,
124	WikiText-103). A reviewer could reasonably question whether these
125	findings reflect architecture-specific artifacts or dataset-specific
126	distributional properties rather than general characteristics of
127	iterative training collapse. We test three orthogonal generalization
128	axes: model scale (Pythia-410M $\to$ Pythia-1.4B, same family, 3.4$\times$
129	parameters), architecture family (Pythia $\to$ OPT-1.3B, different

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We study lead-lag analysis pitfalls in iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M \cite{biderman2023pythia}, a 410M-parameter decoder-only language model.
8	Pythia provides a controlled setting: small enough for multi-seed experiments across five contamination levels, yet large enough to exhibit meaningful generation-over-generation degradation.
9	Cross-architecture validation uses GPT-2-medium (345M parameters, also decoder-only), a second transformer family of similar scale; results are reported in \S\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}, \S\ref{sec:regime}, and \S\ref{sec:discussion}.
10	Training data is drawn from WikiText-103 \cite{merity2017pointer}, using 5{,}000-sample subsets per generation.
11	
12	Our protocol follows the replace paradigm of \citet{shumailov2024curse}, adapted for mixed synthetic-real training at $\alpha{<}1.0$.
13	Each generation $t$ proceeds in three steps: (1)~fine-tune the generation-$(t{-}1)$ model for 5{,}000 steps on a mixture of $\alpha$ fraction synthetic data and $(1{-}\alpha)$ fraction real data (AdamW optimizer, learning rate $5 \times 10^{-5}$, 500 warmup steps, weight decay $0.01$, batch size 4 for Pythia-410M, 32 for GPT-2-medium); (2)~generate 5{,}000 synthetic samples from the fine-tuned model (temperature $1.0$, top-$k{=}50$, max length 512 tokens); (3)~fully replace the synthetic pool.
14	We run $T{=}10$ generations (11 time points including the generation-0 baseline) across contamination levels $\alpha \in \{0.00, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00\}$.
15	The $\alpha{=}1.0$ condition uses four random seeds $\{42, 43, 44, 45\}$; $\alpha{=}0.00$, $\alpha{=}0.25$, and $\alpha{=}0.75$ each use three seeds $\{42, 43, 44\}$; $\alpha{=}0.50$ uses three seeds $\{42, 43, 44\}$.
16	
17	We track two \emph{canary metrics} (cheap-to-compute statistics hypothesized to precede downstream degradation, inspired by early-warning signals in dynamical systems \cite{scheffer2009early}) and three \emph{downstream metrics}.
18	Canary metrics are token entropy (next-token prediction entropy averaged over held-out samples) and expected calibration error (ECE).
19	Downstream metrics are distinct-1 and distinct-2 ($n$-gram diversity \cite{li2016diversity}, computed over all 5{,}000 generated samples per generation) and perplexity (evaluated on held-out real data).
20	Token entropy is the mean next-token prediction entropy $H(p_\theta | \text{context})$ averaged over 1{,}000 held-out samples; ECE uses 15 equal-width bins.
21	We additionally evaluate effective rank and Self-BLEU as candidate canaries (\S\ref{sec:case_study}).
22	Results for distinct-3 are reported in Appendix~\ref{app:distinct3}.
23	Code and data will be released upon publication.
24	
25	All hypothesis tests apply Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR correction at $q{=}0.05$.
26	Permutation Granger causality tests use 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series \cite{shojaie2022granger}.
27	Cross-correlation analysis uses a maximum lag of 5 generations.
28	Table~\ref{tab:drift_summary} summarizes raw metric drift from generation~0 to generation~10 across all five contamination levels.
29

48	
49	\subsection{Method Sensitivity Analysis}
50	\label{sec:method_sensitivity}
51	
52	We open with Claim~3 (method sensitivity) because it conditions the interpretation of all subsequent results.
53	If different statistical methods yield contradictory conclusions on the same data, any single-method analysis is unreliable by construction.
54	
55	\paragraph{Five-method ablation.}
56	We apply five lead-lag detection methods to the $\alpha{=}1.0$ condition (4~seeds, 16 canary$\to$downstream pairs).
57	The fraction of pairs reaching BH-corrected FDR significance varies dramatically by method: naive cross-correlation \textbf{16/16} (100\%), differenced cross-correlation \textbf{14/16} (87.5\%), threshold onset detection \textbf{9/16} (56.3\%), permutation Granger causality \textbf{6/16} (37.5\%), and Toda--Yamamoto Granger \textbf{4/16} (25.0\%).
58	Method choice alone swings significance from \textbf{100\% to 25\%}: 50--57\% of pairs classified as significant under one method flip to non-significant under another.
59	The spurious-signal rate (the fraction of pairs detected by the most liberal method but not confirmed by the most conservative) is \textbf{75\%} across all five methods and \textbf{62.5\%} when excluding Toda--Yamamoto, whose low statistical power at $T{=}10$ inflates this rate (\S\ref{sec:consensus_validation}).
60	Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison} reports significance rates restricted to 8 validated canary$\to$downstream pairs (excluding failed candidates); the 16-pair rates above include effective rank and Self-BLEU to capture the full method-sensitivity landscape.
61	
62	\paragraph{First-differencing diagnostic.}
63	A single preprocessing decision can eliminate an entire analysis channel.
64	For canary$\to$diversity pairs at $\alpha{=}1.0$, all pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing (24/24$\to$24/24), confirming genuine temporal precedence rather than shared-trend confounding.
65	For canary$\to$perplexity, however, undifferenced tests find near-universal significance ($F{=}16$--$36$) while differenced tests find \textbf{zero} significant pairs ($F{\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$; Figure~\ref{fig:differencing_dmax}a).
66	The apparent canary$\to$perplexity relationship is entirely an artifact of co-trending.
67	Notably, at $\alpha{=}0.75$, differenced cross-correlation finds 8/8 pairs significant in \emph{both} forward and reverse directions per seed, demonstrating that this method alone cannot distinguish genuine directional precedence from shared dynamics even after detrending.
68	
69	\paragraph{Augmentation order sensitivity.}
70	Toda--Yamamoto Granger tests show extreme sensitivity to the augmentation order $d_{\max}$ (Figure~\ref{fig:differencing_dmax}b).
71	At $d_{\max}{=}0$ (most permissive), approximately 6\% of pairs are rejected; at adaptive $d_{\max}$ determined by ADF unit-root tests, 75\%; at $d_{\max}{=}1$ (most conservative), \textbf{94\%}.
72	This single hyperparameter swings conclusions from near-universal significance to near-universal non-significance.
73	
74	\begin{figure}[t]
75	\centering
76	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/differencing_dmax.pdf}
77	\caption{First-differencing diagnostic and augmentation order sensitivity (Pythia-410M, $\alpha{=}1.0$, 4~seeds). \textbf{(a)}~First-differencing eliminates the canary$\to$perplexity channel: undifferenced tests find near-universal significance ($F{=}16$--$36$) while differenced tests find zero significant pairs ($F{\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$), exposing these associations as shared-trend artifacts. Canary$\to$diversity pairs retain significance after differencing (24/24$\to$24/24). \textbf{(b)}~Toda--Yamamoto Granger significance rates vary dramatically with augmentation order $d_{\max}$: from $\sim$6\% rejection at $d_{\max}{=}0$ to 94\% at $d_{\max}{=}1$, demonstrating that this single hyperparameter can reverse conclusions about temporal precedence.}
78	\label{fig:differencing_dmax}
79	\end{figure}
80	
81	\paragraph{Inter-method agreement.}
82	Cohen's $\kappa$, averaged over all method pairs, is \textbf{0.185} (near chance; poor agreement); this value is consistent across contamination levels ($\alpha{=}0.0$ through $\alpha{=}1.0$), indicating that method disagreement is a structural property of the analysis rather than a regime-specific artifact.
83	Cross-architecture validation on GPT-2-medium (12 canary$\to$downstream pairs) yields Fleiss' $\kappa{=}0.091$, also in the ``slight agreement'' range, confirming that low inter-method agreement is not architecture-specific.
84	Restricting to three informative methods (excluding naive cross-correlation and Toda--Yamamoto, which anchor the permissive and conservative extremes respectively) raises $\kappa$ to 0.667 (substantial agreement), indicating that the low full-panel $\kappa$ is driven by the deliberate inclusion of methods spanning the entire permissive-to-conservative spectrum rather than by fundamental incoherence among methods.
85	Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison} extends the analysis across contamination levels.
86	At $\alpha{=}0.0$ (no synthetic data, serving as null control), naive cross-correlation finds \textbf{100\%} of pairs significant, a textbook spurious-regression outcome in trending time series.
87	Permutation Granger correctly finds 0\% across all three null-control seeds; Toda--Yamamoto finds 0--12.5\% (1/24 total, attributable to a degenerate $\text{df}_{\text{resid}}{=}1$ case; \S\ref{sec:consensus_validation}).
88	At $\alpha{=}0.50$, naive and differenced cross-correlation both find 100\% significance while threshold onset and all causal methods find 0\% (Figure~\ref{fig:consensus_heatmap}), illustrating that even among non-causal methods, sensitivity diverges sharply at this boundary.
89	
90	\paragraph{Method$\times$regime interaction.}
91	Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison} reveals that the correlation-versus-causal divergence is not uniform across contamination levels.
92	At $\alpha{=}0.25$, differenced cross-correlation drops to 25\% and threshold onset to 0\%, while naive cross-correlation remains at 100\%, demonstrating that even partial detrending removes marginal associations at low contamination.
93	In the lowest-contamination regime ($\alpha{\leq}0.25$, three seeds), the false confirmation rate for the most liberal method approaches 100\%, while all Granger-type tests correctly find 0\%, a regime where single-method analysis is maximally dangerous.
94	The graded response across methods, combined with the block structure visible in Figure~\ref{fig:consensus_heatmap}, motivates the regime-dependent analysis in \S\ref{sec:regime}.
95	
96	\begin{figure}[t]
97	\centering
98	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/consensus_heatmap.pdf}
99	\caption{Method$\times$regime consensus heatmap (Pythia-410M). Each cell shows the consensus tier ($K$-of-$M$ agreement) for a given lead-lag method (rows) and contamination level $\alpha$ (columns). A clear block structure emerges: correlation-based methods (naive, differenced, threshold) find significance across all $\alpha$ levels, while Granger-type methods find significance only at $\alpha{\geq}0.75$. The $\alpha{=}0.50$ column is maximally dangerous (high correlation coexists with zero causal precedence), illustrating Pitfall~3 (regime dependence). Color scale: dark = significant (Tier~3), light = non-significant (Tier~1).}
100	\label{fig:consensus_heatmap}
101	\end{figure}
102

Each cell reports the fraction of 8 canary$\to$downstream pairs (token entropy and ECE $\times$ 4 downstream metrics) reaching BH-corrected significance ($q{=}0.05$), excluding failed candidates (\S\ref{sec:case_study}). Seed coverage: $\alpha{=}1.0$: 4 seeds (32 pairs); $\alpha{=}0.00$, $\alpha{=}0.75$: 3 seeds (24 pairs); $\alpha{=}0.25$ Toda--Yamamoto: 3 seeds (24 pairs), other methods single seed; $\alpha{=}0.50$: 3~seeds for permutation Granger (range shown), single seed for other methods. $^\dagger$Seed-dependent onset: s42${=}$0/8, s43${=}$6/8, s44${=}$3/8 (median 3/8).}

120	
121	
122	\subsection{Regime-Dependent Detectability}
123	\label{sec:regime}
124	
125	Beyond method choice, the contamination level~$\alpha$ determines whether a temporal lead exists to detect.
126	We present regime effects at two levels: raw metric drift (method-independent) and Granger-precedence detectability.
127	
128	\paragraph{Raw drift.}
129	Table~\ref{tab:drift_summary} shows that diversity collapse occurs at \emph{all} contamination levels, not only under full synthetic replacement.
130	Distinct-1 declines by 13.8\% even at $\alpha{=}0.0$ (attributable to fine-tuning on repeated real-data subsets), and more steeply at intermediate contamination: $-40.3{\pm}0.3\%$ ($\alpha{=}0.25$), $-37.1{\pm}0.4\%$ ($\alpha{=}0.50$), $-32.3{\pm}0.5\%$ ($\alpha{=}0.75$), and $-27.8{\pm}1.6\%$ ($\alpha{=}1.0$).
131	The non-monotonic pattern (diversity declining \emph{more} at moderate~$\alpha$ than at extreme~$\alpha$) is confirmed by 3-seed replication at $\alpha{=}0.25$ (std${=}0.3\%$), indicating that this relationship between contamination level and collapse severity is robust rather than a seed-specific artifact.
132	
133	\begin{figure}[t]
134	\centering
135	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/regime_map.pdf}
136	\caption{Detectability regime map across contamination levels (Pythia-410M). Permutation Granger FDR significance rate (solid line) and consensus tier (shaded regions) as a function of $\alpha$. The onset boundary lies at $\alpha{\approx}0.25$--$0.50$: at $\alpha{\leq}0.25$, all seeds agree on null (0/24); at $\alpha{=}0.50$, detectability is seed-dependent (0/8--6/8, 3~seeds, median 3/8); at $\alpha{=}0.75$, directional asymmetry emerges robustly (19/24 forward, 0/24 reverse, 3~seeds) but method consensus remains transitional (Tier~2--3); at $\alpha{=}1.0$, full Confirmatory consensus is achieved (24/24 forward, Tier~3, 4~seeds). Multi-seed replication: $\alpha{=}0.00$, $\alpha{=}0.25$, $\alpha{=}0.50$, $\alpha{=}0.75$: 3~seeds; $\alpha{=}1.0$: 4~seeds.}
137	\label{fig:regime_map}
138	\end{figure}
139	
140	\paragraph{Granger-precedence detectability.}
141	Despite ubiquitous drift, causal detectability is sharply regime-dependent (Figure~\ref{fig:regime_map}).
142	Permutation Granger tests (first-differenced, BH-corrected) find 0/24 pairs FDR-significant at $\alpha{=}0.00$ (3~seeds), 0/24 at $\alpha{=}0.25$ (3~seeds), 0/8--6/8 at $\alpha{=}0.50$ (seed-dependent; 3~seeds, median 3/8), and \textbf{6/8} at $\alpha{=}1.0$.
143	At $\alpha{=}0.75$ (3~seeds), permutation Granger finds 19/24 forward-direction pairs FDR-significant with 0/24 reverse, confirming directional asymmetry; however, method-level consensus varies by seed (Tier~3 pairs: 4, 3, and 1 across seeds), placing this regime in a transitional detection state.
144	Under Bonferroni correction, approximately 3/8 pairs per seed survive, consistent with the transitional nature of the $\alpha{=}0.75$ boundary where marginal signals are sensitive to correction stringency.
145	Within-$\alpha$ multi-seed analysis at $\alpha{=}1.0$ yields \textbf{24/24} canary$\to$diversity pairs FDR-significant across all four seeds, with 0/24 in the reverse direction, confirming temporal asymmetry.
146	The resulting dose-response pattern is monotonic: causal detectability is absent below $\alpha{=}0.25$, seed-dependent at $\alpha{=}0.50$, and robust above $\alpha{=}0.75$ (Figure~\ref{fig:regime_map}).
147	The onset boundary lies at $\alpha{\approx}0.25$--$0.50$, consistent with theoretical expectations about signal-to-noise limits in short time series~\cite{boettiger2012limits}.
148	At the extremes, the transition is unambiguous: 0/24 FDR-significant pairs at $\alpha{\leq}0.25$ (3~seeds) versus 24/24 at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (4~seeds, Tier~3 consensus).
149	Between these extremes, individual-seed stochasticity determines whether the consensus threshold is crossed; the data map a boundary region rather than a sharp phase transition.
150	Sensitivity analysis confirms that treatment/null separation is robust to FDR threshold choice ($q{=}0.01$: 0/24 null, 0/24 treatment at $\alpha{=}0.75$; $q{=}0.10$: 0/24 null, 24/24 treatment; Appendix~\ref{app:robustness}).
151	Cross-architecture validation on GPT-2-medium confirms this boundary: at $\alpha{=}0.25$, at most 1/8 pairs reach Confirmatory consensus ($K{\geq}4/5$), consistent with the null regime, despite substantially stronger canary drift (entropy $-32.8\%$ vs.\ Pythia $-4.7{\pm}0.5\%$), while at $\alpha{=}1.0$ all 6 canary$\to$diversity pairs reach $K{\geq}4/5$ (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
152	Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_regime} summarizes the regime map across both architectures.
153	At $\alpha{=}0.25$, GPT-2-medium yields 1/8 pairs at Tier~3 ($K{\geq}4/5$) and 7/8 at Tier~2 ($K{\geq}3/5$), consistent with Pythia's 0/8 Tier~3, 0/8 Tier~2 result at the same contamination level, placing both architectures in the null/low-detectability regime.
154	
155	\begin{table}[t]
156	\centering
157	\small
158	\caption{Cross-architecture regime map: lead-lag consensus tier and metric drift at $\alpha{=}0.25$ and $\alpha{=}1.0$. Tier~3: $K{\geq}4/5$ methods; Tier~2: $K{\geq}3/5$. Drift is generation~0$\to$10. GPT-2-medium $\alpha{=}0.25$: single seed (seed=42); Pythia $\alpha{=}0.25$: 3-seed mean${\pm}$std. The onset boundary ($\alpha{\approx}0.25$--$0.50$) is consistent across two transformer decoder-only model families of similar scale.}
159	\label{tab:cross_arch_regime}
160	\begin{tabular}{llcccccc}
161	\toprule
162	$\alpha$ & Model & Entropy & ECE & Distinct-1 & Distinct-2 & Perm.\ Granger & Tier~3 \\
163	\midrule
164	0.25 & Pythia-410M  & $-4.7{\pm}0.5$ & $+81.8{\pm}15.9$ & $-40.3{\pm}0.3$ & $-56.4{\pm}0.7$ & 0/8 & 0/8 \\
165	0.25 & GPT-2-medium & $-32.8$ & $+422$ & $-23.5$ & $-31.6$ & 0/8 & 1/8 \\
166	\midrule
167	1.00 & Pythia-410M  & $-60.8{\pm}1.2$ & $+3780{\pm}30$ & $-27.8{\pm}1.6$ & $-34.0{\pm}3.3$ & 6/8 & 6/6 \\
168	1.00 & GPT-2-medium & \multicolumn{6}{c}{(reported in \S\ref{sec:discussion}; all 6 canary$\to$diversity Tier~3)} \\
169	\bottomrule
170	\end{tabular}
171	\end{table}
172	
173	\paragraph{Onset boundary at $\alpha{=}0.50$.}
174	The $\alpha{=}0.50$ regime provides particularly informative evidence.
175	Naive and differenced cross-correlation find 100\% significance while threshold onset finds 0\% (Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison}), and causal methods confirm emerging detectability in a majority of seeds: two of three seeds show directional asymmetry under permutation Granger (6/8 and 3/8 forward, 0/8 reverse), while one seed shows synchronous collapse (0/8).
176	The majority-signal outcome places $\alpha{=}0.50$ in a transitional regime where causal detectability is emerging but not yet robust, bridging the null regime ($\alpha{\leq}0.25$) and the consistently detectable regime ($\alpha{\geq}0.75$).
177	This seed dependence exemplifies our central argument: even within a single regime and method, stochastic variation in training determines whether the consensus threshold is crossed.
178	At this boundary, canary sensitivity is metric-dependent: token entropy fails to reach FDR significance in any seed ($p_{\text{fdr}}{\geq}0.086$), while ECE achieves significance in the two signal-positive seeds (s43, s44), identifying ECE as the more sensitive canary at the onset boundary.
179	Figure~\ref{fig:consensus_heatmap} visualizes this interaction across all method$\times\alpha$ combinations, revealing a clear block structure that separates correlation-based methods from causal methods as a function of regime.
180	
181	
182	\subsection{Case Study: Canary Prediction of Diversity Collapse}
183	\label{sec:case_study}
184	
185	Having established method sensitivity (Claim~3) and regime dependence (Claim~2), we present the conditions under which canary prediction succeeds (Claim~1).
186	We scope this claim precisely: canary metrics predict \emph{diversity collapse} (declining distinct-$n$), not model collapse broadly.
187	
188	\paragraph{Full synthetic replacement ($\alpha{=}1.0$, 4 seeds).}
189	Token entropy exhibits robust Granger-precedence over diversity collapse metrics, confirmed by 4 independent statistical methods (permutation Granger power${=}1.0$), 4 seeds, and cross-architecture replication on GPT-2-medium (Tier~3 consensus; \S\ref{sec:discussion}).
190	Cross-correlation peaks at lag$={+}1$ with $|r|{>}0.99$ (cross-seed standard deviation: 0.0), establishing a consistent one-generation lead.
191	Figure~\ref{fig:metric_trajectory} illustrates the one-generation offset between token entropy decline and distinct-1 decline, visible across all four seeds.
192	Permutation Granger tests (first-differenced) confirm all canary$\to$diversity pairs as FDR-significant across all four seeds, including distinct-3 (Appendix~\ref{app:distinct3}).
193	Reverse-direction tests (downstream$\to$canary) find \textbf{0/24} FDR-significant (all $p{>}0.49$), establishing complete directional asymmetry.
194	
195	\begin{figure}[t]
196	\centering
197	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/metric_trajectory.pdf}
198	\caption{Metric trajectories across 10 generations at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M, 4~seeds shown as individual traces). Token entropy (top, blue) drops sharply at generation~1, while distinct-1 (bottom, red) begins declining at generations~2--3, establishing a visible one-generation canary lead. The consistent lag across all four seeds confirms that the temporal offset is robust rather than seed-specific. Shaded bands show the inter-seed range. This visual pattern corresponds to the statistical finding of 24/24 forward-direction and 0/24 reverse-direction FDR-significant pairs.}
199	\label{fig:metric_trajectory}
200	\end{figure}
201	
202	\paragraph{High contamination ($\alpha{=}0.75$, 3 seeds).}
203	Directional asymmetry is robustly confirmed across three independent seeds (permutation Granger: 19/24 forward pairs FDR-significant, 0/24 reverse).
204	However, method-level consensus varies by seed (Tier~3 pairs: 4, 3, and 1 across seeds 42, 43, 44), placing $\alpha{=}0.75$ squarely in the transitional detection regime.
205	Differenced cross-correlation finds 8/8 pairs significant in \emph{both} forward and reverse directions at each seed, confirming that this method alone cannot distinguish genuine directional precedence from shared dynamics, a seed-level demonstration of Pitfall~1 (\S\ref{sec:pitfalls}).
206	
207	\paragraph{ECE as a conditional secondary canary.}
208	ECE performs equivalently to token entropy across four of five methods (naive, differenced, threshold, permutation Granger).
209	Its classification as ``secondary'' is driven entirely by Toda--Yamamoto Type~II errors at $T{=}10$, where bootstrap power analysis yields estimated power of 0.05--0.20 depending on $d_{\max}$.
210	Resolving ECE's true canary status requires longer time series.
211	
212	\paragraph{Failed canary candidates.}
213	Not all metrics serve as canaries.
214	Effective rank and Self-BLEU show no detectable lead-lag relationship with any downstream metric under any method or contamination level, and are excluded from the consensus framework.
215	Effective rank behavior is model-dependent in an unexpected way: in Pythia-410M, effective rank decreases during collapse (representational contraction), whereas in GPT-2-medium at $\alpha{=}0.25$ it \emph{increases} from 2.37 to 8.48 (+258\%).
216	The GPT-2-medium starting effective rank is near rank-2, suggesting the model's initial representation is already highly concentrated; iterative training disperses it rather than concentrating it further.
217	This opposite directionality across architectures precludes effective rank as a universal canary and motivates architecture-specific analysis (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
218	
219	\paragraph{Consensus filtering.}
220	Of 16 canary$\to$downstream pairs tested at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (4 candidate canaries $\times$ 4 downstream metrics), \textbf{6 survive} the full consensus pipeline ($K{\geq}4/5$ methods, $S{\geq}3/4$ seeds): token entropy and ECE each predicting distinct-1 and distinct-2 (4 pairs; distinct-3 results in Appendix~\ref{app:distinct3} bring the total to~6).
221	The 10 eliminated pairs comprise all canary$\to$perplexity relationships (identified as spurious by the first-differencing diagnostic) and all effective-rank and Self-BLEU pairs (failed canaries).
222	The consensus framework reduces the naive 100\% significance rate to the 37.5\% rate supported by the most conservative individual methods (Figure~\ref{fig:consensus_workflow}).
223	
224	
225	\subsection{Consensus Framework Validation}
226	\label{sec:consensus_validation}
227	
228	We validate the consensus framework's calibration using built-in experimental controls.
229	
230	\paragraph{False positive rate.}
231	At $\alpha{=}0.0$ (three seeds, 24 total pairs), no synthetic data is injected; any detected lead-lag association is a false positive.
232	Individual-method FPR ranges from \textbf{100\%} (naive cross-correlation) through 37.5\% (differenced) and 20.8\% (threshold) down to \textbf{0\%} (permutation Granger: 0/24 across all three seeds).
233	The consensus framework at $K{\geq}4/5$ achieves \textbf{FPR${=}$0\%} (0/24 pairs, three independent seeds), matching the best individual methods while providing robustness against miscalibration of any single method (Table~\ref{tab:fpr_fnr}).
234	
235	\paragraph{False negative rate.}
236	At $\alpha{=}1.0$, we define 6 effect-size reference pairs (token entropy and ECE $\to$ distinct-1/2/3) using criteria independent of the consensus methods: drift ratio ${\geq}3\times$ the $\alpha{=}0.0$ baseline (entropy $11.93\times$, ECE $73.12\times$) and canary onset strictly preceding downstream onset by ${\geq}1$ generation (\S\ref{sec:calibration}).
237	FNR is calibrated on a 4-method panel (excluding differenced cross-correlation to avoid circularity): at $K{\geq}3/4$, $S{\geq}3/4$, all~6 are recovered (\textbf{FNR${=}$0\%}).
238	Toda--Yamamoto alone yields FNR${=}$33.3\% (2/6), missing ECE$\to$distinct-2 and ECE$\to$distinct-3 due to Type~II errors at $T{=}10$.
239	Both FPR and FNR are validated on the joint method${\times}$seed dimension (FPR: 3~seeds at $\alpha{=}0.0$; FNR: 4~seeds at $\alpha{=}1.0$), with cross-seed FPR variance negligible (all three seeds yield 0/8 robust pairs at $K{\geq}4/5$).
240	Table~\ref{tab:fpr_fnr} reports the calibration results.
241	Table~\ref{tab:single-method-errors} complements this analysis by showing the practical consequences of relying on any single method: naive cross-correlation produces false positives at every $\alpha$ including the null control, Toda--Yamamoto misses true signals at $\alpha{=}0.75$, while the consensus at $K{\geq}3$ yields the correct verdict across all five contamination levels.

301	
302	\paragraph{Bootstrap power analysis.}
303	To quantify detection power beyond binary FNR, we perform bootstrap power analysis ($B{=}1{,}000$ resamples from 4-seed data at $\alpha{=}1.0$).
304	Permutation Granger achieves power${=}1.0$ on genuine pairs (canary$\to$diversity) and power${=}0.0$ on spurious pairs (canary$\to$perplexity), providing clean separation.
305	Toda--Yamamoto achieves power${=}0.05$--$0.20$ on genuine pairs (depending on $d_{\max}$), confirming its poor sensitivity at $T{=}10$.
306	We note that bootstrap power${=}1.0$ reflects the effect size in our 4-seed data and should not be interpreted as a universal power guarantee; furthermore, the two methods use different data-generating processes for calibration (permutation Granger resamples observed $\alpha{=}1.0$ trajectories, while Toda--Yamamoto power was estimated under a generic VAR(2) simulation with $\beta{=}0.3$).

329	
330	\subsection{Cross-Architecture, Cross-Scale, and Cross-Domain Generalization}
331	\label{sec:generalization}
332	
333	The methodological pitfalls and consensus framework developed above are calibrated on a single model family (Pythia-410M, WikiText-103).
334	To test whether these findings transfer, we evaluate the consensus pipeline along three generalization axes: architecture family (GPT-2-medium 345M, OPT-1.3B), model scale (Pythia-1.4B, $3.4\times$ parameters within the same family), and training domain (Pythia-1B trained on C4 rather than WikiText-103).
335	All conditions use $\alpha{=}1.0$ and a single seed.
336

341	\label{tab:generalization}
342	\begin{tabular}{llccccccc}

354	
355	\paragraph{Architecture generalization.}
356	Table~\ref{tab:generalization} confirms that the entropy-leads-diversity pattern generalizes across all three decoder-only transformer families tested to date.
357	All models reach Tier~3 consensus ($K{\geq}4/5$) with 6/8 (or 6/6) canary$\to$diversity pairs confirmed.
358	Permutation Granger directionality is perfect across architectures: all forward-direction pairs are FDR-significant while 0 reverse-direction pairs reach significance in any architecture.
359	
360	Method-level variation across architectures is informative.
361	Threshold onset detection reaches 8/8 on OPT-1.3B (the highest single-method score) because OPT's token entropy exhibits a pronounced generation-0$\to$1 discontinuity that cleanly exceeds the onset threshold.
362	Toda--Yamamoto ranges from 0/6 (GPT-2-medium) to 6/8 (Pythia-410M), consistent with its known sensitivity to residual degrees of freedom at short series lengths (\S\ref{sec:consensus_validation}).
363	Despite this cross-method variance, the consensus threshold absorbs it: all three architectures converge at $K{\geq}4/5$.
